# From SQL and Convex

Start with the idiom you already know, then use its Quarry spelling.
Each example below compiles as a complete backend.

```quarry
table accounts:
  principal: principal
  handle: text 3..20 format handle
  bio: text ?
  createdAt: time default now
  unique principal
  unique handle else HANDLE_TAKEN
  read <- .principal = viewer
  insert <- .principal = viewer
  update <- .principal = viewer
  cap 1 by principal
  sync to .principal
mutation saveAccount(handle: text 3..20 format handle, bio: text ?):
  return upsert accounts[principal = viewer] { principal: viewer, handle, bio }
query myAccount():
  return accounts[principal = viewer]
```

| Postgres / RLS idiom | Quarry in the example |
| --- | --- |
| `CREATE TABLE accounts` | `table accounts:`; columns use `name: type`. |
| `NOT NULL` | The default; `handle` is required. |
| Nullable column | `bio: text ?`; omitted optional values are null. |
| `DEFAULT current_timestamp` | `createdAt: time default now`; explicit values win. |
| `PRIMARY KEY` | Every table has an implicit unique `id`; omit it on insert to mint one. |
| `UNIQUE (handle)` | `unique handle else HANDLE_TAKEN`; hidden collisions keep their generic refusal. |
| Bounded text / validation | `text 3..20 format handle`; validated on writes. |
| `CHECK (...)` | No general SQL CHECK clause; use column bounds/formats and `require` in every writer, as below. |
| `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` | `upsert accounts[principal = viewer]`; omitted patch fields stay unchanged. |
| RLS `USING` / `WITH CHECK` | `read <-`, `insert <-`, `update <-`; absent rules deny. |
| `auth.uid()` / `ctx.auth` | `viewer` is the authenticated principal; sessions are handled by the client auth API. |

Quarry has `int`, `time`, `bool`, `text`, `enum`, `decimal S`, `money CUR`,
`json`, `bytes`, bounded lists and asset types; `guide types` shows all of them.
A reference column carries the target table's id type. It does not enforce
existence or cascade deletion; guard required visible targets in the mutation.

## Select, join, page and aggregate

```quarry
table projects:
  title: text
  public
  online only

table members:
  conversation: projects
  principal: principal
  unique conversation, principal
  cap 50 by conversation
  public
  online only

table entries:
  project: projects
  owner: principal
  body: text
  amount: int
  removed: bool default false
  at: time default now
  search body
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- .owner = viewer
  online only

view entryCounts: count entries by .owner
view entryTotals: sum entries of .amount by .owner

query latest(project: projects, c: cursor ?):
  return entries where .owner = viewer and .project = project and not .removed last 20 by .at after c
query expanded(project: projects, c: cursor ?):
  return entries where .owner = viewer and .project = project first 20 by .at after c { *, project: projects[.project] { title } }
query withProject(project: projects, c: cursor ?):
  return entries where .owner = viewer and .project = project and exists projects[.project] first 20 by .at after c { *, project: projects[.project] { title } }
query rosters(c: cursor ?):
  return members where .principal = viewer first 20 by .conversation after c { conversation, roster: members[conversation = .conversation] }
query member(project: projects):
  return exists members[project, viewer]
query selected(owners: [principal] <=8, c: cursor ?):
  return entries where .owner in owners first 20 by .at after c
query totals():
  return { count: entryCounts[viewer], sum: entryTotals[viewer] }
query find(q: text <=100, c: cursor ?):
  return entries where .owner = viewer matching q last 20 by .at after c
mutation add(project: projects, body: text, amount: int):
  require amount >= 0 else NEGATIVE_AMOUNT
  require exists projects[project] else PROJECT_NOT_FOUND
  return insert entries { project, owner: viewer, body, amount }
mutation remove(id: entries):
  update entries[id] { removed: true }
```

| SQL / Convex idiom | Quarry in the example |
| --- | --- |
| `REFERENCES projects(id)` | `project: projects`, not a foreign key: any id string is accepted and nothing cascades; `add` checks the target with `require exists`. |
| `SELECT ... WHERE ... ORDER BY at DESC LIMIT 20` | `latest`: predicate, `last 20 by .at`, then shape if needed. `first` ascends; `id` breaks ties. |
| Keyset pagination | `c: cursor ?` and `after c`; pass the answer's `next` back as `c`. |
| `OFFSET 100` | No offset pagination; keep and follow the opaque cursor. |
| `LEFT JOIN projects` | `expanded` uses `project: projects[.project] { title }`; absent or hidden targets are null. |
| `JOIN projects` | `withProject` adds `exists projects[.project]` before the reference shape; no arbitrary multi-table SQL join syntax. |
| Correlated subquery | `rosters` uses `members[conversation = .conversation]`; the key reads the outer row, while an inner `where` reads the inner row. |
| `EXISTS (...)` | `exists members[project, viewer]`; tests only authorized rows. |
| `IN (...)` | `.owner in owners`; rules still apply independently to every source row. |
| `COUNT(*) ... GROUP BY owner` | `view entryCounts: count entries by .owner`; lookup yields the count, including zero. |
| `SUM(amount) ... GROUP BY owner` | `view entryTotals: sum entries of .amount by .owner`. |
| Full-text / prefix search | `search body` and `matching q`; every search term is a word prefix. |
| Stemming, phrase search, relevance rank | No equivalent; use word-prefix search in a declared column order. |
| Soft delete | `removed: bool default false`, `update`, and `where not .removed`; authorization and physical retention remain your policy. |
| `ctx.db.query().withIndex().order().paginate()` | `latest` states predicates, column order and continuation; the compiler selects the index. |

This select-and-aggregate example runs online so its pages and searches can
choose different orders. For offline pages, choose a sync window and read its
own order newest first; see `guide sync` and N_SYNC_ORDER.

`count(read)` and `sum(read, .amount)` compute a live total without storing a
view; `count(page)` counts that page only. A meter cut never becomes an exact
total. Source rules run before projections, ordering and aggregates reach you.

## Transactions, triggers and background work

```quarry
use server
effect notify

table rooms:
  owner: principal
  public
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  after insert <- exists memberships[room = .id, principal = .owner]
table memberships:
  room: rooms
  principal: principal
  unique room, principal
  cap 50 by room
  public
  insert <- created rooms[.room]
  delete <- exists rooms[id = .room, owner = viewer]
table reminders:
  recipient: principal
  due: time
  expiresAt: time ?
  expire at .expiresAt
  immutable due
  read <- .recipient = viewer
  insert <- .recipient = viewer

view membershipCounts: count memberships by .room
mutation createRoom():
  room = insert rooms { owner: viewer }
  insert memberships { room: room.id, principal: viewer }
  emit notify { room: room.id, principal: viewer }
  return room
mutation setReminder(due: time):
  return insert reminders { recipient: viewer, due, expiresAt: due + 1d }
job deliver(periodStart: time, periodEnd: time):
  for r in reminders where .due >= periodStart and .due < periodEnd by .due:
    emit notify { recipient: r.recipient } once [r.id, periodEnd]
schedule eachMinute every 1m: deliver
action fetchSummary(room: rooms) returns { summary: text }
action dailySummary(periodStart: time, periodEnd: time) returns {} <- viewer = 'service:reporter'
schedule summaries every 1d at 08:00 as service:reporter: dailySummary
```

| SQL / Convex idiom | Quarry in the example |
| --- | --- |
| `BEGIN ... COMMIT` | `mutation createRoom` commits both rows and its emitted effect atomically, or rolls everything back. |
| Trigger-maintained counter | `view membershipCounts: count memberships by .room`; the engine maintains it in each source write. Older `maintain` spelling still compiles. |
| Deferred after-insert check | `after insert <- exists memberships[...]` must hold at the inserting mutation's end; it is a constraint, not an effect handler. |
| Trigger calling an external service | `emit notify` records durable work; an external worker performs it after commit. |
| Cron / scheduled function | `schedule eachMinute every 1m: deliver`; jobs receive period boundaries and chunk automatically. |
| TTL | `expire at .expiresAt`; null never expires, reads hide expired rows before the physical sweep. |
| `ON DELETE CASCADE` | No implicit cascade; delete authorized dependents in the same mutation or use a job for a large lifecycle. |
| Convex `query` | Quarry `query`, as in `myAccount`; may run on a device holding its inputs. |
| Convex `mutation` | Quarry `mutation`, as in `createRoom`; transactions remain metered and rule checked. |
| Convex `action` | Quarry `action fetchSummary`; implement its external work in the server handler. Requires `use server`. |
| Scheduled action | `summaries` invokes `dailySummary` under the named service seat. |

`emit ... once` deduplicates enqueue, not provider delivery. Jobs run as the
system; actions run under their admitted authority. `guide actions`,
`guide jobs` and `guide effects` explain handlers, retries and scheduling.

## Guesses and their one-step repairs

| Guess | Write instead |
| --- | --- |
| `len(x)`, `length(x)` or `x.length` | `count(x)`, `len(x)` and `length(x)` are accepted; `x.length` needs `count(x)`. Characters for text, items for a list or page. |
| `count(*)` | Pass the read: `count(entries where .owner = viewer)`. |
| `limit 20` | `first 20` or `last 20`, with the desired column order. |
| Two `where` modifiers | Accepted as `and` in their written order. |
| `m.helper(x)` | Accepted when the helper is declared in `m.q`; the bare name still works. |
| Two `sync` lines | Keep one audience matching the read rule; a union audience has no equivalent. Use an online-only table when it cannot be inverted. |
| Nullable lookup passed to a required helper | Require it once: `row = table[id] ! else NOT_FOUND`, then pass `row`. |

The canonical counting and required-row spellings are compiled here too:

```quarry
table entries:
  owner: principal
  body: text
  read <- .owner = viewer
fn bodySize(row: entries): count(row.body)
query size(id: entries):
  row = entries[id] ! else NOT_FOUND
  return bodySize(row)
query countMine():
  return count(entries where .owner = viewer)
```

Developer inspection: `snapback4 sql '\dt'` (or `'.tables'`) lists application
tables and maintained views; `sql '\d messages'` (or `'.schema messages'`) shows
Quarry types/nullability, indexes and unique keys. Both include the active schema
generation and bounded stored row counts (exact through 10,000, else >=10,001;
ephemeral unavailable). Use `--json` for structured output, `--release dir` for a
deployment. These read-only commands keep SQLite internals private; see `guide inspect`.
//! Compiled examples first; diagnostic details are under `why`.
pub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Reads

Read authorized rows. `sync … last N` bounds acquisition; cursors page server history (offline: acquired rows).

```quarry
query readForms(roomId: rooms, messageId: messages, c: cursor ?):
  one = messages[messageId]
  must = messages[messageId] ! else NOT_FOUND
  roster = members where .roomId = roomId
  member = exists members[roomId, viewer]
  admin = exists members[roomId = roomId, principal = viewer, role = 'admin']
  return { one, must, roster, member, admin }
query newest(roomId: rooms, c: cursor ?):
  return messages where .roomId = roomId and .body != null last 20 by .at after c
query recentTotal(roomId: rooms, start: time, end: time):
  return count(messages where .roomId = roomId and .at >= start and .at <= end)
```

`one` returns a row/null; `must` refuses missing/hidden rows.
`roster` reads the capped room; `member`/`admin` test membership.
`newest` pages newest-first; `recentTotal` counts under the meter.
Repeated `where` clauses combine with `and` in order.

## Limits

- A positional key names one row; use `where` for populations: E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN.
- Add a cursor if the screen pages: N_NO_CURSOR.
- Unmerged pages with unpinned rule columns cannot certify all callers. N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE names a readable pinned-parent guard; N_NEVER_COMPLETE has none (separate grants may still admit callers).
- A nested read with ambiguous modifiers needs parentheses: E_QUARRY_MODIFIER_SCOPE.
- N_SHAPE_METER: maintained totals (guide views), previews, or queries.

## Details

Unreadable query references act absent: null points or complete empty pages. Mutation references refuse; ids grant no authority.

Reads select authorized rows by id, predicate or search text.
Add an order, page size and cursor to browse a population.

Projections drop `id` unless listed: `{ title }` omits id; `{ id, title }` preserves it. Whole rows retain id.


### Pages and cursors

State the rows, then the order and page. `messages where .roomId = roomId
last 20 by .at after c` means the newest matching messages. The compiler
chooses the index. Positional keys select identity for `id`/same-table references,
otherwise the declared unique key accepting types in order; ambiguous types need named columns.
The rules run before any projection, count or order reaches the caller.
Declare `c: cursor ?`, read `after c`, pass each answer's `next` as `c`; `usePage` does that.

### Completeness

Pinned-parent guards are caller-dependent (`N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE`; `guide recipes`).
`T[]` requests a complete fitting group: `return children[] where .parent = parent`.
It needs authority and a cap/unique bound. Update capped cases that now answer complete.

`complete` means the query answered its declared request within known coverage and execution limits.
Filling a declared page bound is complete.

Every ready `Read` carries `cut: 'meter' | 'words' | null` beside `complete`,
on the server and either device store. An empty page with `cut: 'meter'`
means partial: retry narrower, or continue if `next` exists; never "no matches".
`words` means the first term expanded past 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope: narrow that
term or use scoped search (guide search). If both limits cut, `meter` wins.
Device reads and uniform server reads keep precise reasons.
Non-uniform server reads report `cut: null`: the reason is withheld, with `complete: false` still truthful.
`null` means "not disclosed here", not "not cut"; it also covers a horizon,
missing coverage. For `complete: false` with
`cut: null`, render "Results may be incomplete", even when empty; never "no matches".
A complete empty page has `cut: null` and `complete: true`.
Same-key observations retain their last answer marked `revalidating` during
plain refetch, reconnect and transport failure. Omitted `keep` and `keep: true`
share this policy; `keep: false` withdraws immediately. Inserts and non-policy updates retain through retries; deletes, policy-input changes and unknown evidence start a nonrenewable one-second bound. Bounded failure/timeout withdraws; replacements can still publish.
A notified cut keeps older replica answers ineligible until sync publishes it.
A whole server answer at that cut may replace sooner. Expiry, sign-out,
credential, store, schema and generation resets still withdraw immediately.
`fresh` and `since` describe server freshness, independently of revalidation.
With `keep: true`, a watch also lingers for 30 seconds after its last subscriber
leaves, sharing the same observation, expiry, invalidation and reset fences.
Local commits still revalidate it while unmounted; account/backend changes,
`clear_rows`, `settle_exit`, ephemeral resets and close end its authority.
See `guide client` for the complete retention and screen-lifetime recipe.
A ready list read always carries an array; an absent point can still be null.
`usePage` returns `items: []` while its first page is loading; earlier pages
remain visible while a later page is unavailable.

For device reads, online fallback asks only for missing facts or unknown presence.
Meter/word cuts and cursorless pages stay local, as do held queries and retained left groups.
Predictable pending writes block server fallback for dependent reads; unknown prediction
footprints block every query. Unrelated writes do not block. Withheld speculation
(past-horizon totals, extrema or points) stays `loading`; local cuts stay partial.
For one-shots, see `guide client`.
Keep a screen query locally complete (`snapback4 why <query>`); put server-needing
decorations in their own query so the main list never waits on them.
Keep the screen shell and draft mounted; render current reads.

For an expiring table, return the deadline with each row:

```quarry
query expiringMessages(roomId: rooms, c: cursor ?):
  return messages where .roomId = roomId after c last 20 by .at { id, body, expiresAt }
```

Held device observations republish at `nextExpiry`, including offline; render
those rows directly. Timers follow the row's deadline; a suspended page or a
busy host can delay the callback.
Only server-answered reads need a render-deadline filter to hide a returned
row while its next network answer is pending. Return `expiresAt`, as above.
In React, `useNow(deadline)` owns the timer and responds to clock estimates:

```ts
import { useClient, useNow } from "snapback4/react";
function useExpiringRows<T extends { expiresAt: number | null }>(rows: T[]) {
  const client = useClient();
  const deadline = Math.min(...rows.flatMap(row => row.expiresAt !== null && row.expiresAt > client.now() ? [row.expiresAt] : []));
  const now = useNow(Number.isFinite(deadline) ? deadline : null);
  return rows.filter(row => row.expiresAt === null || row.expiresAt > now);
}
```

Render the rows returned by `useExpiringRows(rows)`. `client.now()` estimates the server's time and never
moves backward; `null` never expires. The hook cleans up on unmount.

For typing and viewing indicators, use an ephemeral table (`guide ephemeral`,
also `guide typing`). Keep its server query separate from the device-held
chats list. The expiring-rows recipe above is for durable disappearing messages.

## Measure live propagation

Mounted reads wake from `/changes`, sync and re-evaluate affected queries, including parent and authorization dependencies.
Measure two signed-in clients with queries mounted: record sender invocation, `sent` acknowledgement, changed `observe` snapshot, React `useLayoutEffect` and image `onLoad` separately.
Acknowledgement follows commit; `waitSeconds: 0` measures custody. Inspect `/changes`, `/sync` and query requests in Network to separate transport from application work.
Measure media separately; repeat 20+ times, idle and loaded.

## Nullable predicates and enum lanes

For a required enum with at most eight variants, `.status != 'done'` opens
the same pinned value lanes as `.status in ['todo', 'doing']`. A bound value
can also select the excluded variant. Fallible expressions remain residual
predicates so their guards still run first. Nullable enums retain a residual
inequality, including null rows; larger enums also stay residual.
Value lanes are chosen only when every lane's prefix decides the source read
rule. Otherwise both `!=` and `in` remain residual filters on one scan, which
can continue through visible rows with `next`; the non-uniform read still
reports `complete: false`.

```quarry
table workTasks:
  owner: principal
  cap 100 by owner
  due: time ?
  status: enum('todo', 'doing', 'done')
  assignee: principal ?
  createdAt: time
  read <- .owner = viewer
  sync to .owner
shape workTaskCard = { id, due }
query overdueTasks(c: cursor ?):
  return workTasks where .owner = viewer and .due < now first 50 by .due after c
query overdueCards(c: cursor ?):
  return workTasks[owner = viewer] first 50 by .due after c where (.due < now) workTaskCard
query openTasks(c: cursor ?):
  return workTasks where .owner = viewer and .status != 'done' first 50 by .createdAt after c
query openTaskValues(c: cursor ?):
  return workTasks where .owner = viewer and .status in ['todo', 'doing'] first 50 by .createdAt after c
query board(status: enum('todo', 'doing', 'done') ?, assignee: principal ?, c: cursor ?):
  return workTasks where .owner = viewer and (status = null or .status = status) and (assignee = null or .assignee = assignee) last 50 by .createdAt after c
```

`check --cost` displays the residual that still tests each visible candidate,
including `in` when another predicate supplies the lanes. A predicate fully
extracted into pins or lanes has no residual-skip clause. These explanations
do not change read rules, meter bounds or completeness.

## Totals, existence and continuation

`count(messages where .roomId = roomId)` and `sum(orders where .owner =
viewer, .amount)` stream their domains. They authorize and filter each row,
then release it. They do not retain a page of bodies. A meter cut or unknown
device coverage leaves the client observation `loading`, never a partial total.
Use a maintained view for a total that should be updated with each write.
`count(page)` still counts only that page.
An unpaged aggregate keeps enum inequalities and membership predicates as
residual filters on its streaming scan. It does not turn them into a bounded
merge page: `count(tasks where .status != 'done')` and
`sum(tasks where .status in ['todo', 'doing'], .n)` return either the whole
authorized total or null on interruption. Scan admission remains unchanged.

`exists messages where .roomId = roomId` stops at a visible match. It accepts
predicates and seeks, but no page clause or cursor. A visible or held witness
proves existence (`true`); absence needs full coverage: an interrupted or
undecided scan answers nothing. Mutations keep their ordinary scan
admission; rule probes keep exact-key semantics.

A resumable read-meter cut after every required head is known can carry `next`
after its last fully shaped emitted row, subject to final-answer eligibility.
Heap exhaustion clears the whole page and leaves the client observation `loading`, with no continuation,
even during the drain. Step exhaustion aborts; output exhaustion can refuse.
A uniform matching scan is always lazy. Opening and word-limit cuts cannot
supply continuation. A terminal full page need not carry `next`. Following a
cursor does not clear earlier incompleteness in `usePage`.

## Queries, mutations and jobs

```quarry
mutation recentPass(roomId: rooms): server
  n = 0
  for row in members where .roomId = roomId first 10 by .principal:
    n = n + 1
  return n
```

A job may put a read-only prelude before its first sweep. It runs again
at the start of every chunk, with its bindings visible there and its work
charged to that chunk's meter. Writes belong inside the sweep body.

Arguments use column types; `c: cursor ?` is an opaque optional cursor.
An omitted optional argument is null. `walk rooms[roomId] up .parentId depth 8`
returns the seed and at most eight parent hops. List operations and helpers
are on `guide language` and `guide shapes`; writes are on `guide writes`.

## Operations

To inspect a real account's server work under loopback `dev`, use the online
client `createClient({ url, session })` (or a bearer `token`); meters are available by default.
Successful `query` and `observe` answers include `Read.meter`:
`{ examined, indexEntries, probes, steps, heapBytes, outputBytes, rowsWritten, cut }`.
`Read.meter.cut` keeps the precise reason even for a non-uniform server read;
it is `meter`, `words` or null, with `meter` winning when both limits cut.
These are the engine's counters for that answer, not latency or process memory;
`probes` is rule_probes and `heapBytes` is charged heap work. A local fallback
keeps its previous answer’s counters only until the fetch settles. Local device reads
have no meter; use the online client to inspect the server for the same account.
`meter: true` and `x-snapback-meter: 1` on `POST /q/<op>` remain explicit forms.
Loopback dev includes the field without either; `serve` ignores the header and never returns
meters. The counters can reflect hidden work and are development-only.
`snapback4 run <op> '<json args>' --as <principal id> --json` also prints
the engine's `metered` counters against `.snapback4/store.sqlite`.
A declared persona such as `alice` resolves to `dev:alice`; any other `--as`
value is used verbatim (including `user:...`, `member:42` and `dev:alice`).
The default is `dev:alice`. Jobs still run as the system.

"##;
//! Compiled examples first; diagnostic details are under `why`.
pub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Shapes

Shapes choose the fields returned from a row or page.
Name a shape to reuse the same result layout across reads.

```quarry
query nestedRoster(c: cursor ?):
  return members where .principal = viewer first 20 by .roomId after c { roomId, roster: members[roomId = .roomId], room: rooms[.roomId] { name } }
```

The outer page selects your memberships.
The named inner key binds that membership's room before reading its roster.
`room` expands the reference into an object, or null when missing or hidden.
Inside each `where`, `.` denotes that read's own row.

## Limits

- Comparing a filtered row column with itself is refused, except optional-column `<=` and `>=` presence tests: E_QUARRY_SELF_EQUALITY.
- Spread a present row or object with known fields: E_QUARRY_SPREAD.
- Write each explicit field once: E_QUARRY_DUPLICATE_FIELD.
- Values joined by branches must have compatible row kinds: E_QUARRY_SHAPE_JOIN.
- Move or narrow a shape that cannot fit the meter: E_SHAPE_EXCEEDS_METER.

## Details

Shapes choose the fields returned from a row or page.
Name a shape to reuse the same result layout across reads.

```quarry
shape brief = { id, body, author: .authorId }                        -- a named shape: bare columns, and `.` for the row

query shapeForms(roomId: rooms, messageId: messages, c: cursor ?):
  page = messages where .roomId = roomId after c last 20 by .at { *, room: rooms[.roomId], count: messageCounts[.roomId] }   -- over a page: `*` spreads every column; a point read inside is null for a hidden row
  row = rooms[roomId] { id, name, tagCount: count(.tags) }           -- over a row: an object; over a nullable row: null when the row is
  named = messages[messageId] brief                                  -- a named shape by its name
  nested = rooms[roomId] { ...card, extra: 1 }                        -- `...name` spreads a named shape's fields
  return { page, row, named, nested }
```

```quarry
table listings:
  title: text
  city: text
  public
table bookings:
  guest: principal
  listing: listings
  start: time
  read <- .guest = viewer
query myBookings(c: cursor ?):
  return bookings where .guest = viewer last 20 by .start after c { *, listing: listings[.listing] { title, city } }
query listingChoice(key: listings, flag: bool):
  p = listings[key] ! else NOT_FOUND
  return ({ ...p } if flag else { ...(p { * }) }) { title: 'override', * }
```

Here `listing` is the expanded object, or null for a missing or hidden listing.

In the first example, table and shape names are the language card's. A shape over a page is
a bounded loop; its fields are the row's columns by bare name, `*` for all
of them, `...shape` for a named shape's fields, and `name: expr` with `.`
as the row. There is no `select` spelling.
A table-typed helper accepts that table's row or reference. A reference's
field reads its row: `.slot.day` is `slots[.slot].day`, null when missing or
hidden; `slots[.slot] ! else NOT_FOUND` refuses instead. Shapes are projections,
not declared helper-argument types.
A row-based shape keeps `pending?: boolean` from its base, including `*`, named
spreads and subsequent shapes. It is this device's unconfirmed change, absent
after confirmation; it is not a schema column or metadata on a containing record.

## Shapes and helpers

`guide reads` owns paging and continuation; `guide programs` owns helpers.
"##;
```

`firstDay` and `lastDay` are calendar-day numbers in the app's chosen calendar;
the last day is excluded. A second guest reserving an occupied night gets
`TAKEN`, and the entire mutation rolls back, including any earlier free nights.
The normal write and work meters bound the loop; exceeding them also rolls
back the mutation. This example only books nights; cancellation must release
its nights in the same transaction as cancelling the booking.

The availability table is deliberately public: a named unique refusal applies
only when the clashing row is readable. A hidden collision stays `E_RULE`,
even with `else TAKEN`. Use this example only if occupied dates and booking
references may be public. Private booking details keep their own read rule.
If the product instead permits readable availability checks, an `exists` or
`count` domain over that public availability table has a uniformly readable
audience. Pinning a private guest merely narrows the check; it cannot enforce
a constraint across all guests.

## On delete restrict

References default to `restrict`; a live child causes E_REFERENCED. Alternatives:
bounded `cascade`, optional `set null`, or `keep`. Cascades recurse under a
cap/unique key per parent; keep/restrict history and audit rows.

## Server-arrival order within a conversation

For timestamp order with ties, use existing columns: `sync last 500 by .at, .id`
and query `last 50 by .at, .id`. No ordinal or conversation-row rewrite is needed.
For server-arrival order, client-minted ids and timestamps are insufficient.
Declare an engine sequence. A client-supplied integer can pin
the history forever by writing the largest value; `sequence by` refuses
such values with E_SEQUENCE_SUPPLIED, including on updates and upserts.

```quarry
table conversations:
  creator: principal
  public 'shared conversation'
  insert <- .creator = viewer
  online only

table members:
  conversationId: conversations
  principal: principal
  joinedAt: sequence by conversationId
  unique conversationId, principal
  public 'shared conversation membership'
  guest: principal
  unique night, guest
  immutable night, guest
  read <- .guest = viewer
  insert <- .guest = viewer
  online only
view rsvpCount: count rsvps by .night
  read <- allow
mutation rsvp(night: nights):
  return insert rsvps { night, guest: viewer }
";

#[test]
fn r2_a_zero_count_guard_on_the_deleted_row_silences_the_delete_note() {
    let guarded = format!("{NIGHTS}mutation cancelNight(night: nights):\n  n = nights[night] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  require n.host = viewer else NOT_HOST\n  require rsvpCount[night] = 0 else HAS_RSVPS\n  delete nights[night]\n");
    let b = clean(&guarded);
    assert!(note(&b, "N_DELETE_RESTRICTED").is_none(), "{:#?}", b.notes);
    // Without the guard the note stays and names the declarations that silence it.
    let bare = format!("{NIGHTS}mutation cancelNight(night: nights):\n  delete nights[night]\n");
    let b = clean(&bare);
    let n = note(&b, "N_DELETE_RESTRICTED").unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{:#?}", b.notes));
    assert!(n.rewrite.contains("`night: nights on delete cascade`") && n.rewrite.contains("any declaration silences this note"), "{n}");
    // A filtered count proves nothing about the other children: the note stays.
    let filtered = NIGHTS.replace("view rsvpCount: count rsvps by .night", "view rsvpCount: count rsvps where .guest != 'x' by .night");
    let b = clean(&format!("{filtered}mutation cancelNight(night: nights):\n  require rsvpCount[night] = 0 else HAS_RSVPS\n  delete nights[night]\n"));
    assert!(note(&b, "N_DELETE_RESTRICTED").is_some(), "{:#?}", b.notes);
    // The guard runs: a guest's hidden RSVP blocks the host's delete.
    let mut engine = engine(&guarded, vec![("nights", vec![("id", id("n1")), ("host", id("hana"))]), ("nights", vec![("id", id("n2")), ("host", id("hana"))])]);
    assert_eq!(mutate(&mut engine, "rsvp", "gus", &[("night", id("n1"))]), "ok");
    assert_eq!(mutate(&mut engine, "cancelNight", "hana", &[("night", id("n1"))]), "HAS_RSVPS");
    assert_eq!(mutate(&mut engine, "cancelNight", "hana", &[("night", id("n2"))]), "ok");
}

#[test]
fn r2_a_not_exists_guard_over_private_children_prints_the_zero_count_and_it_runs() {
    let tables = "table cats:\n  owner: principal\n  immutable owner\n  read <- allow\n  delete <- .owner = viewer\n  online only\ntable sightings:\n  cat: cats\n  reporter: principal\n  immutable cat, reporter\n  read <- .reporter = viewer\n  insert <- .reporter = viewer\n  cap 500 by cat\n  online only\nmutation report(cat: cats):\n  return insert sightings { cat, reporter: viewer }\n";
    let natural = format!("{tables}mutation removeCat(cat: cats):\n  c = cats[cat] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  require c.owner = viewer else NOT_OWNER\n  require not exists sightings[cat = cat] else HAS_SIGHTINGS\n  delete cats[cat]\n");
    let b = bound(&natural);
    let d = b.diagnostics.iter().find(|d| d.code == "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT").unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{:#?}", b.diagnostics)).clone();
    let quoted: Vec<&str> = d.rewrite.split('`').skip(1).step_by(2).collect();
    assert!(d.rewrite.starts_with("First add `.reporter = viewer`"), "the direct audience predicate leads: {d}");
    assert!(d.rewrite.contains("an absence check over every sightings row"), "the zero-count alternative remains: {d}");
    let view = quoted.iter().find(|q| q.starts_with("view ")).unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{d}"));
    let check = quoted.iter().find(|q| q.starts_with("require ")).unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{d}"));
    assert_eq!((*view, *check), ("view sightingsCount: count sightings by .cat", "require sightingsCount[cat] = 0 else HAS_SIGHTINGS"), "{d}");
    let repaired = format!("{tables}{view}\n  read <- allow\nmutation removeCat(cat: cats):\n  c = cats[cat] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  require c.owner = viewer else NOT_OWNER\n  {check}\n  delete cats[cat]\n");
    let b = clean(&repaired);
    assert!(note(&b, "N_DELETE_RESTRICTED").is_none(), "the applied rewrite also silences the delete-site note: {:#?}", b.notes);
    let mut engine = engine(&repaired, vec![("cats", vec![("id", id("tom")), ("owner", id("olive"))]), ("cats", vec![("id", id("kit")), ("owner", id("olive"))])]);
    assert_eq!(mutate(&mut engine, "report", "rae", &[("cat", id("tom"))]), "ok");
    assert_eq!(mutate(&mut engine, "removeCat", "olive", &[("cat", id("tom"))]), "HAS_SIGHTINGS", "rae's sighting is hidden from olive and still counts");
    assert_eq!(mutate(&mut engine, "removeCat", "olive", &[("cat", id("kit"))]), "ok");
}

// ---- #1c: a branch on a pin-decided parent probe is a page guard (LLP 3000.036 §16.3) ----

#[test]
fn a_parent_probe_guard_beside_a_grant_is_followed_and_the_runtime_agrees() {
    // Lane 088: staff, the garden's owner (a parent hop) and the visitor, with
    // the owner's arm written as an `if` on the identical keyed probe.
    let schema = "table staff:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable gardens:\n  owner: principal\n  immutable owner\n  read <- allow\n  online only\ntable checkins:\n  garden: gardens\n  visitor: principal\n  at: time\n  immutable garden, visitor\n  read <- .visitor = viewer or .garden.owner = viewer or exists staff[viewer]\n  online only\n";
    let query = "query gardenVisits(garden: gardens, c: cursor ?):\n  if exists staff[viewer]:\n    return checkins where .garden = garden first 50 by .at after c\n  if exists gardens[garden, owner = viewer]:\n    return checkins where .garden = garden first 50 by .at after c\n  return checkins where .garden = garden and .visitor = viewer first 50 by .at after c\n";
    let source = format!("{schema}{query}");
    let b = clean(&source);
    assert!(notes(&b, "N_NEVER_COMPLETE").is_empty(), "every arm is decided for the callers who reach it: {:#?}", b.notes);
    let rows = || -> Rows {
        vec![
            ("staff", vec![("id", id("st1")), ("principal", id("sam"))]),
            ("gardens", vec![("id", id("g1")), ("owner", id("gwen"))]),
            ("checkins", vec![("id", id("k1")), ("garden", id("g1")), ("visitor", id("vic")), ("at", Value::Int(1))]),
            ("checkins", vec![("id", id("k2")), ("garden", id("g1")), ("visitor", id("val")), ("at", Value::Int(2))]),
        ]
    };
    let mut guarded = engine(&source, rows());
    let pairs = [("garden", id("g1")), ("c", Value::Null)];
    assert_eq!(page(&mut guarded, "gardenVisits", "sam", &pairs).0, true, "the grant decides the staff page");
    let (complete, ids) = page(&mut guarded, "gardenVisits", "gwen", &pairs);
    assert_eq!((complete, ids.len()), (true, 2), "the owner's page is decided by the pinned parent probe");
    assert_eq!(page(&mut guarded, "gardenVisits", "vic", &pairs), (true, BTreeSet::from(["k1".to_owned()])));
    // The unguarded page merges the visitor's branch with the guards (LLP
    // 3000.036.001): complete for everyone. On one index (arithmetic in the
    // predicate) it is complete for staff and the owner, capped for a visitor.
    let merged = format!("{schema}query visits(garden: gardens, c: cursor ?):\n  return checkins where .garden = garden first 50 by .at after c\n");
    assert!(note(&clean(&merged), "N_NEVER_COMPLETE").is_none());
    let mut all = engine(&merged, rows());
    assert_eq!(page(&mut all, "visits", "vic", &pairs), (true, BTreeSet::from(["k1".to_owned()])));
    let open = format!("{schema}query visits(garden: gardens, c: cursor ?):\n  return checkins where .garden = garden and .at + 1 > 0 first 50 by .at after c\n");
    let b = clean(&open);
    let n = note(&b, "N_NEVER_COMPLETE").unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{:#?}", b.notes));
    assert!(n.message.contains("for callers `exists staff[viewer] or ") && n.message.contains("is decided by the scan's pins, so the server decides the page once"), "{n}");
    let mut unguarded = engine(&open, rows());
    assert_eq!(page(&mut unguarded, "visits", "gwen", &pairs), (true, BTreeSet::from(["k1".to_owned(), "k2".to_owned()])));
    assert_eq!(page(&mut unguarded, "visits", "vic", &pairs), (false, BTreeSet::from(["k1".to_owned()])));
}

/// The parent probe decides the page only when its holders can read the row

exec
/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '287,390p' llp/3000.036.002-intent-aware-lints-and-bootstrap.rfc.md
sed -n '1,235p' snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/print.rs
sed -n '35,130p' snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/ast.rs
sed -n '250,520p' snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/parser/expr.rs" in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-lang
 succeeded in 0ms:
## 9. Wave 12 declaration safety and diagnostic fidelity

Quarry's binder-supplied expression inputs are reserved at every binding
declaration. `viewer`, `now`, `commit` and `new` cannot name an operation or
helper parameter, a local, or a loop variable. Refusal at the declaration is
E_QUARRY_RESERVED with a concrete rename. This is a front-end safety boundary:
the builtins keep their existing meaning and type, and no runtime lookup,
shadowing rule, IR operation or wire value changes. The reservation prevents a
valid-looking declaration from being ignored when expression binding resolves
one of these names intrinsically.

`use identity`, `use identity password` and `use identity guests` declare one
project-wide method set. A second declaration is E_QUARRY_SYNTAX at the later
site, whether it is in the same file or another file. The refusal names both
method sets and the first site. It never merges or replaces methods, so source
order and file order cannot silently change the authentication surface.

Diagnostics recognize two SQL habits without taking an ordinary language
name. `count table where predicate` says aggregates take parentheses and emits
`count(table where predicate)`. After project-wide named-shape resolution, an
unresolved trailing `desc` says `last N by .column` is already descending and
removes the suffix; a declared `shape desc` remains an ordinary shape. Tests
apply each emitted text to the diagnosed source and compile the result. When
no compatible declared key exists, a positional value whose static type is a
row/reference from another table leads with that mismatch (`secrets` cannot
key `rolls`) only if no relevant declared key accepts that table at the same
position; the repair names the declared position's expected type. Compatible
secondary keys and foreign values that fit their own composite-key position
retain ordinary ambiguity/type advice. A bad `search` field is a
fault of that field: retain the table and its valid columns in the diagnostic
catalog, omit only the unusable search entry, and avoid cascading unknown-table
errors. This recovery affects rejected-source diagnostics only.

A concrete named-update source rewrite always binds both `old` and `next`.
When the rejected unnamed rule already says `old.field`, preserve it; map a
bare `.field` in that same rule to `next.field`, while field-looking text in
string literals remains unchanged. The golden catalogue applies the whole
rewrite and recompiles, including these mixed-image cases. A
multi-line `require` lookup skips layout tokens when finding its following
`else CODE`, so its one-line repair keeps the author's exact code.

E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT leads with a direct `.principalColumn = viewer` predicate
when the refused scan is nonuniform and one read-rule disjunct makes that the
smallest qualifying repair. Uniform expiry-specific refusals retain their
expiry repair. The
heavier point, maintained-total and constraint designs remain after it.
N_UNREADABLE_PROBE names a prior positive, dominating readable dependency on
the require/read path when one exists and states when implication is still
unproved; negated or disjunctive existence does not qualify. Fixture
argument drift is silent for every authored refusal expectation: a concrete
code and the generic `denied`, `refused` and `failed` forms may intentionally
carry invalid arguments. Malformed case structure and non-refusal expectations
still receive N_FIXTURE_ARGUMENT_DRIFT. The sweep summary calls derived
require failures "probe refusals on derived arguments (expected)".

A maintain may record an intentional aggregate delta channel with
`disclosure reviewed 'rationale'`. The rationale must be nonempty, changes no
read/write authority or runtime schema, and suppresses N_MAINTAIN_DELTA only
for that maintain. Without it, the note offers the annotation alongside the
existing restriction choices. N_DELETE_RESTRICTED points directly to the
`On delete restrict` guide section, which distinguishes keep, cascade, set-null
and restrict behavior across multi-level chains.

Acceptance covers each declaration entry, same-file and cross-file identity,
both SQL repairs, cross-table keys, invalid-search recovery, mixed old/next
rewrites, multi-line refusal codes, direct viewer advice, require/read context,
reviewed disclosure, every refusal expectation, derived-refusal output and the
delete-guide pointer. These changes preserve execution, authorization,
admission, completeness, storage and protocol semantics.
//! Quarry text from syntax, for source-aware diagnostics and pasteable rewrites.
use crate::ast::*;

type Source<'a> = dyn Fn(&Expr) -> Option<(String, bool)> + 'a;

/// Rewrite callers may embed an expression in another operator or clause.
/// Use the same precedence renderer as source excerpts, with a grouped root.
pub fn expression(expr: &Expr) -> String {
    let grouped = match expr {
        Expr::Binary { .. } | Expr::Unary { .. } | Expr::If { .. } => true,
        Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } | Expr::Created { read, .. } =>
            read.filter.is_some() || read.matching.is_some() || read.range.is_some(),
        _ => false,
    };
    with_source_at(expr, if grouped { 8 } else { 0 }, &|_| None)
}

pub(crate) fn with_source(expr: &Expr, source: &Source<'_>) -> String { with_source_at(expr, 0, source) }
pub(crate) fn with_source_at(expr: &Expr, minimum: u8, source: &Source<'_>) -> String { render(expr, minimum, source) }

pub fn quote(value: &str) -> String {
    format!("'{}'", value.replace('\\', "\\\\").replace('\'', "\\'").replace('\n', "\\n").replace('\t', "\\t"))
}

fn priority(expr: &Expr) -> u8 {
    match expr {
        Expr::Binary { op, .. } => crate::parser::precedence(op).unwrap_or(0),
        Expr::If { .. } | Expr::Read(_) | Expr::Exists { .. } | Expr::Created { .. } | Expr::Merge(_) | Expr::Write { .. } | Expr::Walk { .. } => 0,
        Expr::Unary { .. } => 7,
        Expr::Assert { .. } | Expr::Shape { .. } | Expr::ShapeUse { .. } | Expr::Member { .. } => 8,
        _ => 9,
    }
}

fn render(expr: &Expr, minimum: u8, source: &Source<'_>) -> String {
    let wrap = |text: String, grouped: bool| if priority(expr) < minimum && !grouped { format!("({text})") } else { text };
    if let Some((text, grouped)) = source(expr) { return wrap(text, grouped); }
    let sub = |e| render(e, 0, source);
    let text = match expr {
        Expr::Name { name, .. } => name.clone(),
        Expr::Focal { .. } => ".".into(),
        Expr::Literal { value, .. } => match value {
            Literal::Str(s) => quote(s), Literal::Number(n) => n.clone(),
            Literal::Bool(b) => b.to_string(), Literal::Null => "null".into(),
        },
        Expr::Array { items, .. } => format!("[{}]", items.iter().map(sub).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
        Expr::Object { fields, .. } => format!("{{ {} }}", object_fields(fields, source)),
        Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } => {
            let value = render(value, 7, source);
            if op == "not" { format!("not {value}") }
            else if value.starts_with('-') { format!("-({value})") } else { format!("-{value}") }
        }
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } => {
            let p = priority(expr);
            let (l, r) = if op == "??" { (p + 1, p) } else { (p, p + 1) };
            format!("{} {op} {}", render(left, l, source), render(right, r, source))
        }
        Expr::Member { base, name, .. } => match base.as_ref() {
            Expr::Focal { .. } => format!(".{name}"),
            other => format!("{}.{name}", render(other, 8, source)),
        },
        Expr::Call { name, args, .. } => format!("{name}({})", args.iter().enumerate().map(|(n, arg)| {
            let value = sub(arg);
            if n > 0 && matches!(name.as_str(), "day" | "hour" | "bucket") && matches!(arg, Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Number(_), .. }) && source(arg).is_none() { format!("{value}ms") }
            else { value }
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
        Expr::Read(read) => read_with(read, source),
        Expr::Merge(merge) => format!("merge {}{}{} {} {} by {}{}:", merge.table, merge.matching.as_ref().map_or(String::new(), |q| format!(" matching {}", sub(q))), merge.cursor.as_ref().map_or(String::new(), |c| format!(" after {}", sub(c))), if merge.order == Order::Asc { "first" } else { "last" }, merge.take, merge.index, merge.filter.as_ref().map_or(String::new(), |p| format!(" where {}", sub(p)))),
        Expr::Exists { read, .. } => format!("exists {}", read_with(read, source)),
        Expr::Created { read, .. } => format!("created {}", read_with(read, source)),
        Expr::Assert { value, code, message, .. } => format!("{} ! else {code}{}", render(value, 8, source), message.as_deref().map(|m| format!(" {}", quote(m))).unwrap_or_default()),
        Expr::ShapeUse { base, name, .. } => format!("{} {name}", render(base, 8, source)),
        Expr::Shape { base, fields, .. } => format!("{} {{ {} }}", render(base, 8, source), shape_with(fields, source)),
        Expr::Walk { table, seed, column, depth, .. } => format!("walk {table}[{}] up .{column} depth {depth}", sub(seed)),
        Expr::If { then, condition, otherwise, .. } => format!("{} if {} else {}", render(then, 1, source), render(condition, 1, source), sub(otherwise)),
        Expr::Write { op, table, keys, fields, if_absent, condition, .. } => {
            let mut out = format!("{} {table}", op.word());
            // `delete row` / `update row { … }` keep their row-variable spelling.
            if *op != WriteOp::Insert && !keys.is_empty() { out.push_str(&format!("[{}]", keys.iter().map(|k| key_part(k, source)).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "))); }
            if *op != WriteOp::Delete { out.push_str(&format!(" {{ {} }}", object_fields(fields, source))); }
            if *if_absent { out.push_str(" if absent"); }
            if let Some(condition) = condition { out.push_str(&format!(" if {}", sub(condition))); }
            out
        }
    };
    wrap(text, false)
}

pub fn read_expr(read: &ReadExpr) -> String { read_with(read, &|_| None) }

fn read_with(read: &ReadExpr, source: &Source<'_>) -> String {
    if let Some((text, _)) = source(&Expr::Read(read.clone())) { return text; }
    let sub = |e| render(e, 0, source);
    if !read.order_keys.is_empty() && read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some()) {
        let mut predicates = read.keys.iter().map(|key| format!(".{} = {}", key.column.as_ref().unwrap(), render(&key.value, 5, source))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        predicates.extend(read.seeks.iter().map(|bound| format!(".{} {} {}", bound.column, bound.op, render(&bound.value, 5, source))));
        if let Some(filter) = &read.filter { predicates.push(render(filter, if predicates.is_empty() { 0 } else { 3 }, source)); }
        let mut out = read.table.clone();
        if !predicates.is_empty() { out.push_str(&format!(" where {}", predicates.join(" and "))); }
        if let Some(matching) = &read.matching { out.push_str(&format!(" matching {}", sub(matching))); }
        if let Some(range) = &read.range { out.push_str(&format!(" {} {}", if range.order == Order::Asc { "first" } else { "last" }, range.take)); }
        out.push_str(&format!(" by {}", read.order_keys.iter().map(|key| format!(".{key}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")));
        if let Some(cursor) = read.range.as_ref().and_then(|range| range.cursor.as_ref()) { out.push_str(&format!(" after {}", sub(cursor))); }
        return out;
    }
    let mut out = format!("{}[{}]", read.table, read.keys.iter().map(|key| key_part(key, source)).chain(read.seeks.iter().map(|b| format!("{} {} {}", b.column, b.op, sub(&b.value)))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "));
    if let Some(matching) = &read.matching { out.push_str(&format!(" matching {}", sub(matching))); }
    if let Some(range) = &read.range {
        if let Some(cursor) = &range.cursor { out.push_str(&format!(" after {}", sub(cursor))); }
        out.push_str(&format!(" {} {}", if range.order == Order::Asc { "first" } else { "last" }, range.take));
    }
    if let Some(index) = &read.index { out.push_str(&format!(" by {index}")); }
    if let Some(filter) = &read.filter { out.push_str(&format!(" where {}", sub(filter))); }
    out
}

fn key_part(part: &KeyPart, source: &Source<'_>) -> String {
    let value = render(&part.value, 0, source);
    match &part.column { Some(column) => format!("{column} = {value}"), None => value }
}

fn object_fields(fields: &[ObjectField], source: &Source<'_>) -> String {
    fields.iter().map(|field| match &field.value {
        Some(value) if field.spread => format!("...{}", render(value, 8, source)),
        Some(value) => format!("{}: {}", field.name, render(value, 0, source)), None => field.name.clone(),
    }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")
}

fn shape_with(fields: &[ShapeField], source: &Source<'_>) -> String {
    fields.iter().map(|field| match field {
        ShapeField::Spread { .. } => "*".to_owned(), ShapeField::NamedSpread { name, .. } => format!("...{name}"),
        ShapeField::Bare { name, .. } => name.clone(),
        ShapeField::Field { name, value, .. } => format!("{name}: {}", render(value, 0, source)),
    }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")
}

pub fn scalar_type(type_: &ScalarType) -> String {
    let base = match &type_.kind {
        TypeKind::Cursor => "cursor".to_owned(),
        TypeKind::Text { min, max, format } => {
            let mut out = match min {
                Some(min) => format!("text {min}..{max}"),
                None => format!("text <={max}"),
            };
            if let Some(format) = format {
                out.push_str(&format!(" format {format}"));
            }
            out
        }
        TypeKind::Id => "id".into(),
        TypeKind::Principal => "principal".into(),
        TypeKind::Int => "int".into(),
        TypeKind::IntRange { min, max } => snapback4_core::schema::int_range_text(*min, *max),
        TypeKind::Decimal { scale } => format!("decimal {scale}"),
        TypeKind::Money { currency, scale } => format!("money {currency} {scale}"),
        TypeKind::Time => "time".into(),
        TypeKind::Bool => "bool".into(),
        TypeKind::Json { max } => format!("json <={max}"),
        TypeKind::Bytes { max } => format!("bytes <={max}"),
        TypeKind::Image { max, once } => format!("image <={max}{}", if *once { " once" } else { "" }),
        TypeKind::Video { max } => format!("video <={max}"),
        TypeKind::File { max, types } if types.is_empty() => format!("file <={max}"),
        TypeKind::File { max, types } => format!("file <={max} ({})", types.iter().map(|t| format!("'{t}'")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
        TypeKind::Enum { variants } => format!("enum({})", variants.iter().map(|v| format!("'{v}'")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
        TypeKind::Ref { table } => table.clone(),
        TypeKind::List { item, min: 0, max } => format!("[{}] <={max}", scalar_type(item)),
        TypeKind::List { item, min, max } => format!("[{}] {min}..{max}", scalar_type(item)),
    };
    if type_.optional { format!("{base} ?") } else { base }
}
}

impl ScalarType {
    pub fn required(&self) -> ScalarType {
        ScalarType { kind: self.kind.clone(), optional: false }
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct KeyPart {
    pub column: Option<String>,
    pub value: Expr,
    pub site: Site,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ShapeField {
    Spread { site: Site },
    NamedSpread { name: String, site: Site },
    Bare { name: String, site: Site },
    Field { name: String, value: Expr, site: Site },
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ShapeDecl {
    pub name: String,
    pub fields: Vec<ShapeField>,
    pub site: Site,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct FunctionDecl {
    pub name: String,
    pub args: Vec<ArgDecl>,
    pub body: FunctionBody,
    pub site: Site,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum FunctionBody {
    Expression(Expr),
    Block(Vec<Stmt>),
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Literal {
    Str(String),
    Number(String),
    Bool(bool),
    Null,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Order {
    Asc,
    Desc,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct RangeSpec {
    pub order: Order,
    pub take: u32,
    pub cursor: Option<Box<Expr>>,
    pub site: Site,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct SeekBound {
    pub column: String,
    pub op: String,
    pub value: Expr,
    pub site: Site,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ReadExpr {
    /// Original prefix length, only for a normalized explicit cursor page.
    pub cursor_prefix: Option<usize>,
    /// Computed lane keys introduced by the planner, never written as fields.
    pub computed: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, Expr>,
    /// Column order in the intent spelling; an index name remains `index`.
    pub order_keys: Vec<String>,
    pub table: String,
    pub keys: Vec<KeyPart>,
    pub seeks: Vec<SeekBound>,
    pub filter: Option<Box<Expr>>,
    pub index: Option<String>,
    pub range: Option<RangeSpec>,
    /// `matching q`: the search text, read through a `words(..)` index whose
    /// word component follows exactly the keys.
    pub matching: Option<Box<Expr>>,
    pub site: Site,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct MergeLane {
                    else {
                        read.matching = Some(Box::new(self.expression(0, &["after", "first", "last", "by", "where", "<newline>", ":", "{", ",", "}", ")", "]", "<probe-shape>"])?));
                        if self.maybe("by") { read.index = Some(self.word()?.value); }
                    }
                    self.diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_MODIFIER_SCOPE",
                        format!("{modifier} could modify the inner or outer read; parenthesize the inner read, or move the modifier before `where`"),
                        format!("({})", print::read_expr(read))).guide("reads").mechanical());
                }
                break;
            }
            // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — SQL-habit refusals carry pasteable Quarry rewrites.
            // SQL writes `count table where …`; Quarry makes the counted
            // domain an ordinary parenthesized expression.
            if matches!(&value, Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "count")
                && self.at_kind(Kind::Word)
                && self.peek_at(1).value == "where"
            {
                let Expr::Name { site, .. } = &value else { unreachable!() };
                let site = site.clone();
                let table = self.word()?.value;
                let read = ReadExpr {
                    cursor_prefix: None,
                    computed: Default::default(),
                    order_keys: Vec::new(),
                    table,
                    keys: Vec::new(),
                    index: None,
                    range: None,
                    matching: None,
                    filter: None,
                    seeks: Vec::new(),
                    site: site.clone(),
                };
                let read = self.parse_range(read, self.position - 1, false)?;
                return Err(syntax_with(
                    &site,
                    "aggregates take parentheses: `count(table where …)`",
                    format!("count({})", print::read_expr(&read)),
                )
                .guide("from-sql"));
            }
            if self.at("?") && self.peek_at(1).value == "." {
                let site = self.take("?")?.site;
                self.take(".")?;
                self.word()?;
                // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.5 — ordinary field access is null-propagating and evaluates its base once.
                return Err(Diagnostic::new(&site, "E_QUARRY_UNSUPPORTED", "?. is not part of Snapback 4; field access already returns null for a null base", ".").mechanical());
            } else if self.at(".") && self.peek_at(1).kind == Kind::Word {
                let site = self.take(".")?.site;
                let name = self.word()?.value;
                value = Expr::Member { base: Box::new(value), name, site };
            } else if self.at("(") && call_name(&value).is_some() {
                let (name, name_site) = call_name(&value).expect("a helper name");
                let site = self.take("(")?.site;
                if name == "count" && self.at("*") {
                    let rewrite = self.count_star_rewrite();
                    if self.peek_at(1).value == ")" { self.register_repair(&name_site, start..self.position + 2, &rewrite, None); }
                    let mut refusal = unsupported(&name_site, "count(*)", rewrite).guide("from-sql");
                    if refusal.rewrite.is_empty() { refusal.message.push_str("; pass the table or filtered read to count"); }
                    self.position += 1;
                    self.take(")")?;
                    self.diagnostics.push(refusal);
                    value = Expr::Call { name, args: Vec::new(), site };
                    continue;
                }
                let mut args = Vec::new();
                while !self.at(")") {
                    args.push(if name == "bucket" && args.len() == 1 { self.maintain_duration(false)? }
                        else if matches!(name.as_str(), "day" | "hour" | "bucket") && args.len() == if name == "bucket" { 2 } else { 1 } { self.maintain_duration(true)? }
                        else { self.expression(0, &[",", ")"])? });
                    if !self.maybe(",") {
                        break;
                    }
                }
                self.take(")")?;
                value = Expr::Call { name, args, site };
            } else if self.at("[") && matches!(value, Expr::Name { .. }) {
                let Expr::Name { name, site } = value else { unreachable!() };
                value = Expr::Read(self.parse_read(&name, &site)?);
            } else if matches!(value, Expr::Name { .. }) && matches!(self.peek().value.as_str(), "first" | "after" | "last" | "matching" | "by" | "where") && !stops.contains(&self.peek().value.as_str()) {
                // `t first N by index`: the whole table's page, the empty
                // prefix spelled without brackets, as `chunk` takes it.
                let Expr::Name { name, site } = value else { unreachable!() };
                value = Expr::Read(ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), table: name, keys: Vec::new(), index: None, range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new(), site });
            } else if matches!(value, Expr::Name { .. } | Expr::Read(_)) && self.at("limit") && self.peek_at(1).kind == Kind::Number {
                let site = self.take("limit")?.site;
                let n = self.number()?;
                return Err(unsupported(&site, "limit", format!("first {n} — ascending; use last {n} for descending order")).guide("reads"));
            } else if matches!(value, Expr::Read(_)) && self.at("before") {
                return Err(unsupported(&self.peek().site.clone(), "before", "use after c; reverse display order in the client when needed"));
            } else if matches!(value, Expr::Read(_)) && matches!(self.peek().value.as_str(), "matching" | "after" | "first" | "last" | "by" | "where") {
                let Expr::Read(read) = value else { unreachable!() };
                let read = self.parse_range(read, start, stops.contains(&"<probe-shape>"))?;
                value = Expr::Read(read);
            } else if self.maybe("!") {
                let assertion = self.tokens[self.position - 1].site.clone();
                if !self.maybe("else") {
                    return Err(syntax_with(&assertion, "presence assertions require a refusal code", format!("{} ! else NOT_FOUND", print::expression(&value))));
                }
                let code = self.word()?.value;
                let message = if self.at_kind(Kind::Str) { Some(self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?.value) } else { None };
                value = Expr::Assert { value: Box::new(value), code, message, site: assertion };
            } else if self.at("{") {
                let site = self.take("{")?.site;
                let table = expression_table(&value).map(str::to_owned).unwrap_or_default();
                let fields = self.with_focal(&table, |parser| parser.parse_shape_fields())?;
                self.take("}")?;
                value = Expr::Shape { base: Box::new(value), fields, site };
            } else if self.at_kind(Kind::Word) && !stops.contains(&"<probe-shape>") && precedence(&self.peek().value).is_none() && !stops.contains(&self.peek().value.as_str()) && !matches!(self.peek().value.as_str(), "else" | "for" | "in" | "if" | "absent") {
                let named = self.word()?;
                value = Expr::ShapeUse { base: Box::new(value), name: named.value, site: named.site };
            } else {
                break;
            }
        }
        Ok(value)
    }

    pub(super) fn parse_read(&mut self, table: &str, site: &Site) -> Fallible<ReadExpr> {
        self.take("[")?;
        let mut keys = Vec::new();
        let mut seeks = Vec::new();
        while !self.at("]") {
            let key_site = self.peek().site.clone();
            let mut column = None;
            let mut end = 1;
            if self.at_kind(Kind::Word) {
                while self.peek_at(end).value == "." && self.peek_at(end + 1).kind == Kind::Word { end += 2; }
            }
            if self.at_kind(Kind::Word) && self.peek_at(end).value == "!=" {
                return Err(syntax_with(&key_site,
                    "probe brackets take named equalities, not != tests",
                    "pin a full unique key and compare the row field outside the brackets, or use a bounded program `exists table[key] where .column != value`; program probes see only caller-readable rows, while table-rule probes see all rows; use unique/cap/after insert constraints for invariants across hidden rows"));
            }
            if self.at_kind(Kind::Word) && self.peek_at(end).value == "=" {
                column = Some(self.maintain_column()?);
                self.take("=")?;
            }
            if column.is_none() && self.at_kind(Kind::Word) && matches!(self.peek_at(end).value.as_str(), ">" | ">=" | "<" | "<=") {
                let column = self.maintain_column()?;
                let op = self.peek().value.clone();
                self.position += 1;
                let value = self.expression(0, &[",", "]"])?;
                seeks.push(SeekBound { column, op, value, site: key_site });
            } else {
                if !seeks.is_empty() { return Err(syntax_with(&key_site, "pin the prefix before the seek comparisons", "t[prefix, col >= start, col < end] first 50 by index")); }
                let value = self.expression(0, &[",", "]"])?;
                keys.push(KeyPart { column, value, site: key_site });
            }
            if !self.maybe(",") {
                break;
            }
        }
        self.take("]")?;
        Ok(ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), table: table.to_owned(), keys, index: None, range: None, filter: None, matching: None, seeks, site: site.clone() })
    }

    pub(super) fn parse_range(&mut self, mut read: ReadExpr, start: usize, probe: bool) -> Fallible<ReadExpr> {
        let enclosing = self.focal_table.clone();
        let diagnostic_start = self.diagnostics.len();
        let mut predicates = Vec::new();
        let mut order = None;
        let mut take = None;
        let mut cursor: Option<Box<Expr>> = None;
        let site = self.peek().site.clone();
        let mut seen = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
        let mut violation: Option<Token> = None;
        // Non-index modifiers delimit the predicate; a bare inner `by` needs explicit scope.
        let stops = ["matching", "after", "first", "last", "by", "where", "limit", "<newline>", ":", "{", ",", "}", ")", "]"];
        let mut value_stops = stops.to_vec();
        value_stops.push("<probe-shape>");
        let mut predicate_stops = stops.to_vec();
        predicate_stops.push("<where>");
        while matches!(self.peek().value.as_str(), "matching" | "after" | "first" | "last" | "by" | "where") {
            let key = self.word()?;
            let rank = match key.value.as_str() {
                "matching" => -1,
                "after" => 0,
                "first" | "last" => 1,
                "by" => 2,
                _ => 3,
            };
            if violation.is_none() && rank != 3 && seen.contains(&rank) {
                violation = Some(key.clone());
            }
            seen.insert(rank);
            match key.value.as_str() {
                "where" => {
                    let where_start = self.position - 1;
                    let predicate = self.filtered_predicate(&read.table, &predicate_stops, false)?;
                    predicates.push((where_start..self.position, predicate.clone()));
                    read.filter = Some(Box::new(match read.filter.take() {
                        Some(previous) => Expr::Binary { op: "and".into(), left: previous, right: Box::new(predicate), site: key.site },
                        None => predicate,
                    }));
                }
                "matching" => {
                    // The search text: a page over the words of a `words(..)`
                    // index the text's terms are prefixes of.
                    let candidate = self.expression(0, &value_stops)?;
                    if read.matching.is_none() {
                        read.matching = Some(Box::new(candidate));
                    }
                }
                "after" => {
                    let candidate = self.expression(0, &value_stops)?;
                    if cursor.is_none() {
                        cursor = Some(Box::new(candidate));
                    }
                }
                "first" | "last" => {
                    let candidate = self.number()?;
                    if take.is_none() {
                        order = Some(if key.value == "first" { Order::Asc } else { Order::Desc });
                        take = Some(candidate);
                    }
                }
                _ => {
                    if self.maybe(".") {
                        read.order_keys.push(self.word()?.value);
                        // @ref LLP 3000.036#11 — inside an object a comma may end the page: `{ notes: t where … by .at, votes: v }`.
                        while self.at(",") {
                            let next = self.peek_at(1).clone();
                            if next.value == "." {
                                self.take(",")?;
                                self.take(".")?;
                                read.order_keys.push(self.word()?.value);
                            } else if next.kind == Kind::Word && matches!(self.peek_at(2).value.as_str(), ":" | "," | "}") {
                                break;
                            } else if next.kind == Kind::Word {
                                let keys = read.order_keys.iter().map(|key| format!(".{key}")).chain(std::iter::once(format!(".{}", next.value))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                                return Err(syntax_with(&next.site, format!("each order key starts with a dot; got `{}`", next.value), format!("by {keys}")));
                            } else {
                                break;
                            }
                        }
                    } else {
                        let candidate = self.word()?.value;
                        if read.index.is_none() { read.index = Some(candidate); }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        if let Some(violation) = violation {
            return Err(syntax_with(&violation.site, format!("read modifier {} is repeated; choose one value", violation.value), "write each modifier once: [matching q] [after c] first|last N by index [where predicate]"));
        }
        if take.is_none() && cursor.is_none() {
            self.finish_read_repair(&read, start, &predicates, enclosing.as_deref(), diagnostic_start, probe);
            return Ok(read);
        }
        if probe && take.is_none() {
            // Retain the cursor long enough for exists/created to give their
            // plain-read rewrite instead of suggesting a page inside a probe.
            read.range = Some(RangeSpec { order: Order::Asc, take: 1, cursor, site });
            self.finish_read_repair(&read, start, &predicates, enclosing.as_deref(), diagnostic_start, probe);
            return Ok(read);
        }
        let (Some(take), Some(order)) = (take, order) else {
            return Err(syntax_with(&site, "range reads require first N or last N", format!("{} first 50", print::read_expr(&read))));
        };
        read.range = Some(RangeSpec { order, take, cursor, site: site.clone() });
        self.finish_read_repair(&read, start, &predicates, enclosing.as_deref(), diagnostic_start, probe);
        Ok(read)
    }

    pub(super) fn parse_merge(&mut self) -> Fallible<MergeExpr> {
        let site = self.take("merge")?.site;
        let table = self.word()?.value;
        let matching = if self.maybe("matching") { Some(Box::new(self.expression(0, &["after", "first", "last", "by", ":"])?)) } else { None };
        let mut cursor = None;
        if self.at("before") {
